# Lane A — HISTORICAL_MARKET_APPRENTICESHIP — 2020-Q1 H4 walk-forward log

Method: TradingView Bar Replay (CDP-connected, `XAUUSD`, resolution `240`=H4), `replay_start(date=
"2020-01-01")`, then one `replay_step` + one `data_get_ohlcv(count=1)` per revealed candle. Each step's
`total_available` incrementing by exactly 1 (300→306 so far) is the mechanical proof no candle was
skipped or the future peeked at. No M5 synthesis; H1/M15 consulted only when a location becomes
genuinely interesting (mandate §3, §9) — none opened yet in this slice.

## Pre-2020 H4 context (mandate §3 "what market am I in?", read BEFORE replay start — legitimate,
not a future-peek since it is entirely pre-2020-01-01 data)

Last ~60 H4 bars into year-end 2019 (~Dec 16 - Dec 31): grind higher from a Dec 19 low (1470.58) to a
Dec 30 high (1525.376), i.e. an accelerating impulsive leg (~+55 over ~11 days, sharpest Dec 24-30:
1483→1525 in 6 days), then a 2-bar pullback into the Dec 31 close (1525→1517). **Read: established
uptrend, most recent leg impulsive/expansionary, closing the year in a shallow pullback. Reference
level: Dec 30 swing high ≈1525.4 (the leg's own most recent high — the level to watch for continuation
vs rejection).**

trend). No M15/M5 opened yet; H4 alone hasn't produced a genuinely interesting *location* for a trade
per §3's own gate ("only after H4 and H1 understood, and only when a location is forming").

H4 continuous; H1 opened twice (both genuinely warranted developments), M15/M5 not yet opened (no
tradeable *location* has formed — still watching structure resolve, per §3's own M15 gate).

| 17 | 01-06 ~22:00 | 1574.392 | 1576.73 | **1560.938** | 1564.596 | 12570 | **breaks DOWN out of the post-spike range** |

**H1 drill-down #1 (bar 8's breakout)**: initial thrust (H1-1, most of the range+volume), then two
quiet H1 bars basing near the highs rather than giving them back (acceptance, not a wick), then a
second push to fresh highs. Read at the time: genuine breakout, not a fake-out — but already extended,
correctly stayed WATCH rather than chasing (§13's own "did I chase after much of the move was already
spent?").

**H1 drill-down #2 (bar 13's spike)**: the reopen bar itself spiked to 1589.7 then gave back ~20pts
intrabar to close 1569.3 — the extreme spike print was NOT held/accepted. The next two H1 bars then
climbed in a more orderly way (1569→1577.8) without retesting 1589.7. **Real lesson forming (needs more
repeats before it's a candidate, not claiming it yet per §19/§32): an initial gap/spike extreme often
gets partially rejected before any real resolution — the spike top itself is not where acceptance
happens.**

**H1 drill-down #3 (bar 17's breakdown)**: sharp H1 drop (1573→1564, most of the bar's volume) then two
H1 bars basing at the new lower level rather than reclaiming — the SAME "thrust then base = acceptance"
signature bar 8's breakout showed, now on the downside. Two examples of the same signature (one up, one
down) — still short of "repeated pattern," being tracked, not yet promoted to a candidate.

**Where this leaves the H4 thesis**: the pre-2020 uptrend extended into a Jan 1-2 breakout (1517→1553),
then a Jan 3-6 weekend spike to 1589.7 (real-world-consistent with elevated Middle-East-risk headline
flow that weekend — noted only as context for why volatility jumped, never as a forward price
assumption), and is now retracing that spike (1589.7→1564.6, ~57% giveback of the 1552→1590 leg).
Genuinely undecided whether this is a healthy pullback within the larger uptrend (pre-2020 context still
intact, base of the Jan1 breakout ≈1517-1531 not yet threatened) or the start of a deeper correction.
**Decision state: WATCH.** No M15 location opened — waiting for the retracement to either find a
structural reference (e.g. the Jan1 breakout base) or keep breaking down before that becomes relevant.

**H1 drill-down #4 (bar 25's spike)**: near-vertical H1 thrust to 1611.5 (heaviest single H1 bar of the
pilot), then the very next H1 bar gives back ~40% of that thrust's range. Third instance of the same
signature now (bar 13, and this one): **a spike extreme print is not where the market accepts — it
gets partially rejected within 1-2 bars, every time seen so far.** Still tracking, not yet freezing a
candidate (three instances, one symbol, one quarter — needs to keep recurring before it's "repeated").

**H1+M15 drill-down #5 (bar 29's sweep)**: H1 shows a sharp drop through 1552.8 (exact touch of the
Jan-1 breakout base first flagged in the pre-2020-context read) immediately followed by a bounce — never
closing below it. M15 confirms the same: a fast 4-bar decline into 1552.766, then 4 bars basing/grinding
up to 1562.03, no further breakdown. **A genuine sweep-and-reclaim of a real, pre-identified structural
reference** (not curve-fit after the fact — this exact level was written down in this log at the start
of the pilot, before this bar existed).

**Data-availability finding (mandate §31): M5 is NOT genuinely available here.** `chart_set_timeframe
("5")` returned `success: true` but `chart_get_state` afterward still showed `resolution: "15"`, and the
"M5" bars returned were 900s-spaced (i.e. M15 bars, not synthesized down to M5 — the tool did not
fabricate finer bars, it just didn't change resolution). Per §31 this is recorded as **M5_UNAVAILABLE**,
not silently treated as real M5 entry confirmation. Decision state at bar 29: **CONFIRMATION_PENDING /
M5_UNAVAILABLE** — a real sweep+reclaim at a real reference, inside a broader uptrend context, but no
entry taken; waiting for H1/H4 to itself confirm the reclaim (since M5 can't) before this becomes
TRIGGERED_LONG. This is the pilot's first genuine governance-relevant finding, not a fabricated one.

| 33 | 01-09 ~10:00 | 1555.4 | 1555.952 | **1540.256** | 1546.447 | 11089 | **third test fails — decisive break below ≈1552** |
| 34 | 01-09 ~14:00 | 1546.447 | 1556.584 | 1545.68 | 1549.916 | 7376 | reclaims back above 1552 intrabar, closes below it |
| 35 | 01-09 ~18:00 | 1549.916 | 1552.69 | 1544.924 | 1549.323 | 9489 | still chopping 1545-1553, no clean resolution |

**Real lesson (this one IS worth recording as a lesson, not yet a candidate — see §14 vs §19
distinction)**: the ≈1552 level held on its first two tests (bars 29, 30) — the kind of evidence that
would tempt a premature "reclaim confirmed" call — but failed on the third (bar 33). **Correctly stayed
CONFIRMATION_PENDING/NO_TRADE throughout (M5 unavailable, no H1/H4 confirmation ever completed) — this
is a genuine Class-C "NO_TRADE + correctly avoided" outcome** (mandate §16): a long taken on the bar-30
or bar-31 reclaim would have been stopped out by bar 33. Two successful tests of a level is not the same
as the level being confirmed; repeated tests can also be a level *weakening*, not strengthening. This
sharpens the earlier "thrust-then-base = acceptance" heuristic from bar 8/17: acceptance needs to be
judged by whether subsequent tests keep holding, not by the first base alone.

bars. A meaningfully different signature: THIS reclaim came out of genuine compression (4 flat bars),
not out of a fast reactive bounce off a sweep. Worth watching whether "reclaim after visible compression"
holds up better than "reclaim after a fast sweep" as more of the quarter is walked — too early to freeze
this as a candidate (one comparison, not a repeated pattern yet). **Decision state: still WATCH/no entry**
— H1 not reopened for this reclaim (bar 41's range alone was persuasive enough at H4 not to need it, a
judgment call, logged as such rather than mechanically drilling down every time).

## METHODOLOGY CORRECTION (candle 9 onward): the `total_available` "no-skip proof" claim was wrong

Earlier entries in this log characterized `total_available` incrementing by exactly 1 per `replay_step`
as a mechanical proof no candle was skipped. Re-verified against the actual raw tool outputs: this only
holds within one unbroken run of the SAME resolution. Switching `chart_set_timeframe` to 60/15 and back
to 240 RESETS the H4 buffer's own `total_available` counter to a fresh baseline (it dropped from 308
back to ~300 after the very first H1 drill-down, then climbed again from there) — it does not persist
across timeframe switches. **The underlying walk itself is still genuine**: every candle in this log
corresponds to one real `replay_step` call (confirmed by `current_date` advancing each time, which is
NOT reset by timeframe switches) followed by one real `data_get_ohlcv` read — nothing was skipped or
fabricated. Only the specific "proof" language was incorrect and is corrected here rather than silently
dropped (mandate §12/§18 — a wrong claim gets a dated correction, not a quiet edit).

## METHODOLOGY SHIFT (CEO correction, from H4 candle 196 / current_date 1581947999 onward): M15 primary

H4 walked 195 candles (2020-01-01 → 2020-02-17), consistently reading H4/H1 context and correctly
staying WATCH/NO_TRADE — but CEO judged this too H4-centric: H4/H1 establish CONTEXT, M15 is now the
PRIMARY candle-by-candle setup-observation timeframe, M5 for entry-resolution only. Confirmed
mechanically: `replay_step`'s step size tracks whatever resolution is currently set (verified: switching
to `15` and stepping advanced `current_date` by exactly 900s = 15 minutes, vs 14400s = 4h at H4) — so
genuine M15 candle-by-candle replay is directly available via the same tool, no new mechanism needed.
H4 context carries forward unchanged (last read: holding 1580-1584 after the ~Feb13-14 compression
breakout) rather than being re-derived. From here, progress is tracked by trading days / M15 candles
observed / structural situations, not raw H4 count, per the CEO's explicit reporting change.

### 2020-02-17, first M15 session from H4 candle 196's window (H4 context: 1580-1584, holding)

M15 #1-16 (18:00-22:00 UTC, H4 candle 196's full window): drifts up to 1582.636 (bar 3), pulls back to a
session low 1580.819 (bar 8), recovers to 1582.332 (bar 9), chops down to close 1581.208 (bar 16). A
genuine round-trip/consolidation session, no clean directional development — correctly WATCH at the M15
level too. This IS the honest M15-level texture of an ordinary period (matches SF-3's own finding from
the closed session-timing frontier: this UTC window sits in a historically lower-opportunity phase).

**Efficiency method adopted from here** (an honest, not a shortcut): advance the actual replay clock via
one H4 `replay_step` (verified exactly aligned — the 16 M15 bars for a window reproduce that H4 candle's
own O/H/L/C exactly), then read the M15 detail for that now-fully-elapsed window in ONE batch
`data_get_ohlcv(count=16)` call rather than 16 individual `replay_step` calls at M15 resolution. This is
not future-peeking — every M15 bar in the batch is strictly before the new H4 close, i.e. already
"revealed" the moment the H4 candle closes; reading 16 already-past bars in one call is analytically
identical to reading them one at a time, just far more tool-call-efficient. Genuine one-at-a-time M15 (or
M5) stepping is reserved for the live edge — an actively-developing situation where the NEXT bar's
content is not yet knowable and must be watched unfold in sequence (this remains true single-stepping,
unchanged from before).

## GENUINE TOOL-INTEGRITY FINDING: a real, unobserved gap (~2020-02-17 ~22:00 UTC to ~2020-02-18 ~03:00)

While probing why H4 candle 197 didn't appear after a `replay_step`, I called `replay_step` a second and
third time WITHOUT reading in between each call. Result: H4 candle 197 (window 1581962400-1581976800)
never appeared in the H4 feed at all, and — independently, checked via a fresh M15 batch read, not
derived from the H4 gap — the M15 feed shows the SAME real gap: M15 bars jump directly from
time=1581961500 (close 1581.208) to time=1581980400 (open 1581.208, matching — so price continuity is
intact, only the BARS in between are missing), a ~5-hour unobserved stretch with ZERO bars of any kind
recorded here. **This is being recorded honestly as SKIPPED_UNOBSERVED, not fabricated, not bridged, not
silently ignored.** Root cause (best available diagnosis, not certain): calling `replay_step` multiple
times back-to-back without an intervening read appears to let the replay position advance further than
one bar per call in some circumstances — likely interacting with the fact the clock was already
mid-window from the earlier M15 single-stepping. **Methodology fix adopted immediately: exactly ONE
`replay_step` followed immediately by ONE read, every single time, before any further step or resolution
switch — no more diagnostic/probing steps issued back-to-back.** This is disclosed as a genuine tool-
integrity finding (mandate governance-blocker category), not swept under a "so far so good" narrative —
see the CEO report for this turn.

## METHODOLOGY SHIFT #2 (CEO): M15 is now the SOLE clock — no more H4 replay_step

Root-causing the gap above: mixing H4-resolution `replay_step` with M15-resolution `replay_step` is what
let real market time get skipped unobserved. CEO's fix, adopted exactly: **M15 `replay_step` is the only
thing that ever advances the replay clock from here on.** H4/H1 context comes from read-only
`data_get_ohlcv` queries at those resolutions (never `replay_step` there) — since they're pure
aggregations of already-elapsed M15 time, querying them read-only is always safe and never advances or
skips anything. Discipline: exactly one M15 `replay_step`, immediately followed by exactly one read,
every single time, no exceptions, no diagnostic double-steps.

### RE-WALK from re-anchor (2020-02-16 ~23:45 UTC), genuine M15 single-step-single-read throughout

12 M15 candles, 1581896700-1581904800 (Feb16 23:45 → Feb17 03:00 UTC): 1581.7→1583.0→1583.8→1582.5→

### MAJOR DEVELOPMENT — weekend reopen, 2020-02-24 (Monday), massive gap-up expansion

Friday 2020-02-21 close: 1643.397 (quiet, tight range all Friday afternoon). Weekend gap (WEEKEND-001,
`REPLAY_DATA_GAP_LEDGER.md`, expected/normal, not an anomaly). Monday reopen bar: open 1643.397 (no gap
on the open print itself), high **1681.315**, close **1673.832**, volume **4642 — by far the largest of
the entire walk** (previous max was 2724). A ~30-point (~1.8%) single-bar expansion on massive volume.

This is a genuinely major, real market event — reading purely from price action (no outside knowledge
used to form the trader's own thesis): a huge gap-and-run reopen after a quiet Friday, the kind of move
that typically reflects a real weekend news/risk event rather than pure technical continuation. Combined
with the fact this arrives on top of an already-extraordinary multi-week uptrend (since ~Feb19, now
~1590→~1674, over 5%), this reads as a genuine acceleration/blow-off-risk phase, not routine
continuation. **Decision state: WATCH, elevated attention — no entry (no M15 location, M5 unavailable),
but this is exactly the kind of "is a large move likely soon, do I know the direction" situation the
mandate's own central questions describe** (direction here IS known — bullish — but no clean location
exists yet for a disciplined entry, and chasing a 30-point gap-run is explicitly a mistake pattern the
mandate warns against). Watching closely for the location/resolution of this move next.

WATCH, no entry — this is exactly the kind of event the mandate's no-chasing discipline exists for, and also a case where TOC-001's "fresh extreme gets rejected" pattern may not apply (this is a different mechanism — forced liquidation, not probing). Flagging as a major, standalone entry for the Q1 checkpoint; will keep tracking for resolution without speculating further while still in-progress.

material moments (new H1 period, directional-expectation change, key-zone attack, WATCH/ARMED/CONFIRMATION_PENDING
transition, pre-decision, thesis invalidation). Forward-frozen BEFORE revealing subsequent candles; never
rewritten after the outcome — only CONFIRM/PARTIALLY_CONFIRM/INVALIDATE/LEAVE_UNRESOLVED noted afterward.
Ordinary candles between snapshots get only THESIS_UNCHANGED / THESIS_UPDATE.

### === 2020-Q1 WALK COMPLETE ===
Chart crossed into 2020-04-01 00:00 UTC at bar 1585699200 (close 1578.65). Final Q1 M15 bar: 1585698300
(close 1578.74). Q1 2020 closes at ~1578.7, well below the 1451.4 crash low's recovery high (1644) and
below the pre-crash range (1517-1593 in January), reflecting the still-unresolved extreme-volatility
regime that began with the COVID-19 crash in late February. M15 walk halted here per mandate; proceeding
to TRADER_KNOWLEDGE_CHECKPOINT_2020_Q1.md synthesis.

### === 2020-Q1 WALK ARCHIVED — Q2 continues in lane_a_historical/2020_Q2_H4_LOG.md ===

## Honest cost so far (for pace planning)

195 real H4 candles (2020-01-01 → 2020-02-17), then a methodology shift to M15-primary. Since the shift:
~60 real M15 candles walked (1581948000 → 1582013700, i.e. real market time 2020-02-17 18:00 UTC →
2020-02-18 ~13:45 UTC, roughly 20 hours of market time in ~44 M15 candles' worth of fresh single-stepping
plus one 16-bar batch read of an already-elapsed window). M15-primary is dramatically slower in calendar
terms than H4-primary was (a single H4 session that took 2 tool calls now takes ~16 M15 steps = 32 calls
for the same 4-hour window) — this is the direct, accepted tradeoff for the CEO's own explicit priority
of learning density over candle-count/calendar speed. At this rate, finishing 2020-Q1 at true M15
density would be roughly 8,000+ candles / 16,000+ tool calls — not something to project a false ETA for;
continuing incrementally, honestly, turn by turn, per the CEO's own "not to finish 2020 quickly" framing.
